When it happens, I usually just have to take a moment and pause to steady myself before I can get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences been affecting your ability to work or your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I'm still showing up for the most essential meetings, of course, but... I've actually had to delegate a lot of my community obligations lately. And I've been shortening my workdays, too. I just find that I'm not quite as steady, or really as effective with people as I normally am, so I've had to step back a bit where I can.\n\nDoctor: How long has it been since your cousin died and you started experiencing these sleep disturbances and hospital images?\n\nPatient: It's been about three weeks. That's when... well, that's the night my cousin died in the highway collision, and everything just started right after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your reactions to your cousin's death are stronger than what others around you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to answer that. I mean, I haven't really been comparing myself to anyone else. I just know that this is how I'm feeling and it's... it's been difficult to manage. I haven't really talked to the rest of the family about how they're sleeping or what they're seeing in their heads. I'm just trying to get through my own days right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. I mean... I haven't actively avoided the highway or any specific places that remind me of him or the accident. I know I mentioned earlier that I've been pulling back from some of my work and community obligations, but that's really just because I'm so exhausted and unfocused right now. It's not because those activities remind me of what happened. So, no, I haven't been avoiding things for that reason.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I end up going into board meetings feeling like I’ve only slept in pieces, and I just can't keep functioning like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these hospital images during the day as well, not just at night?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It happens at odd moments during the day. Especially if... well, if I hear a phone ring late, or if I see the glare of headlights on the highway. A really sharp picture of the hospital corridor will just push right into my mind, or I'll hear the police officer’s voice again. When it happens, I usually just have to take a moment and pause to steady myself before I can get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences been affecting your ability to work or your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I'm still showing up for the most essential meetings, of course, but... I've actually had to delegate a lot of my community obligations lately. And I've been shortening my workdays, too. I just find that I'm not quite as steady, or really as effective with people as I normally am, so I've had to step back a bit where I can.\n\nDoctor: How long has it been since your cousin died and you started experiencing these sleep disturbances and hospital images?\n\nPatient: It's been about three weeks. That's when... well, that's the night my cousin died in the highway collision, and everything just started right after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your reactions to your cousin's death are stronger than what others around you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to answer that. I mean, I haven't really been comparing myself to anyone else. I just know that this is how I'm feeling and it's... it's been difficult to manage. I haven't really talked to the rest of the family about how they're sleeping or what they're seeing in their heads. I'm just trying to get through my own days right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. I mean... I haven't actively avoided the highway or any specific places that remind me of him or the accident. I know I mentioned earlier that I've been pulling back from some of my work and community obligations, but that's really just because I'm so exhausted and unfocused right now. It's not because those activities remind me of what happened. I'm just stepping back from my usual routine because I'm so drained, not because I'm trying to avoid reminders.\n\nDoctor: Are you making an effort to push away or suppress thoughts or memories of your cousin's death when they come to mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. Like I said, I'm not staying away from anyone or any specific places because they remind me of him. I'm just exhausted, so I'm not doing as much, but it's really not about avoiding reminders.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to recall specific details about the night your cousin died?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, and that part really frightens me. I mean, I can account for all the funeral arrangements and the phone calls I made afterward... but there are parts of the initial police call, and what exactly happened when I first reached the hospital, that are just... simply blank. I can't remember them at all, and that is very much not like me.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding talking or thinking about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not. I'm just trying to get through my own days right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. I mean... I haven't actively avoided the highway or any specific places that remind me of him or the accident. I know I mentioned earlier that I've been pulling back from some of my work and community obligations, but that's really just because I'm so exhausted and unfocused right now. It's not because those activities remind me of what happened. So, no, I haven't been avoiding things for that reason.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific things that you've found yourself avoiding because they remind you of your cousin or the accident?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying, no, not really. There aren't any specific things I'm staying away from because they remind me of him or the accident. I haven't been avoiding the highway or anything like that. I'm just stepping back from my usual routine because I'm so drained, not because I'm trying to avoid reminders.\n\nDoctor: Are you making an effort to push away or suppress thoughts or memories of your cousin's death when they come to mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. I mean, when the thoughts or memories come up, I don't try to force them out of my head. They just... they just kind of happen. I'm not making any conscious effort to block them or push them away.\n\nDoctor: Are there any people, places, or things that you're avoiding because they remind you of your cousin or the accident?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just went over this, doctor... but no, there really aren't. Like I said, I'm not staying away from anyone or any specific places because they remind me of him. I'm just exhausted, so I'm not doing as much, but it's really not about avoiding reminders.\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to recall specific details about the night your cousin died?\n\nPatient: Yes, actually, and that part really frightens me. I mean, I can account for all the funeral arrangements and the phone calls I made afterward... but there are parts of the initial police call, and what exactly happened when I first reached the hospital, that are just... simply blank. I can't remember them at all, and that is very much not like me.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding talking or thinking about your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not. I think this goes back to what I was saying a few minutes ago... I'm really not trying to avoid the topic. If I need to talk about it with family or sort out arrangements, I do. I don't shy away from discussing it or thinking about it when it comes up. It's just the reality of the situation right now.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific activities or situations that you've found yourself avoiding because they might trigger memories or feelings related to your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I'm really not. I know we keep circling back to this idea of avoidance, but... no, there aren't any activities I'm steering clear of. If a situation comes up or something needs to be handled, I just deal with it. I'm not dodging anything to spare my feelings, I'm just trying to get through the days.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your overall functioning and ability to take care of daily responsibilities have been significantly impacted since your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I mean, I’m still showing up for the most essential meetings—I have to keep things running. But I’ve definitely had to delegate my community obligations, and... well, I've been shortening my workdays. I’m just not as steady or as effective with people as I normally am right now. So, yes, it has impacted my day-to-day capacity.\n\nDoctor: Are there any memories or thoughts about your cousin's death that you're trying not to think about?\n\nPatient: No, I'm not. I know we're touching on this again, but... really, there aren't any specific memories or thoughts I'm trying to block out. If a thought about what happened comes into my head, it just is what it is. I'm not actively trying to push anything out of my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

I end up going into board meetings feeling like I’ve only slept in pieces, and I just can't keep functioning like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these hospital images during the day as well, not just at night?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. It happens at odd moments during the day. Especially if... well, if I hear a phone ring late, or if I see the glare of headlights on the highway. When it happens, I usually just have to take a moment and pause to steady myself before I can get back to what I was doing.\n\nDoctor: Have these experiences been affecting your ability to work or your relationships with others?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has. I'm still showing up for the most essential meetings, of course, but... I've actually had to delegate a lot of my community obligations lately. And I've been shortening my workdays, too. I just find that I'm not quite as steady, or really as effective with people as I normally am, so I've had to step back a bit where I can.\n\nDoctor: How long has it been since your cousin died and you started experiencing these sleep disturbances and hospital images?\n\nPatient: It's been about three weeks. That's when... well, that's the night my cousin died in the highway collision, and everything just started right after that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your reactions to your cousin's death are stronger than what others around you are experiencing?\n\nPatient: I don't really know how to answer that. I mean, I haven't really been comparing myself to anyone else. I just know that this is how I'm feeling and it's... it's been difficult to manage. I haven't really talked to the rest of the family about how they're sleeping or what they're seeing in their heads. I'm just trying to get through my own days right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of your cousin's death?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. I mean... I haven't actively avoided the highway or any specific places that remind me of him or the accident. I know I mentioned earlier that I've been pulling back from some of my work and community obligations, but that's really just because I'm so exhausted and unfocused right now. It's not because those activities remind me of what happened. I'm just stepping back from my usual routine because I'm so drained, not because I'm trying to avoid reminders.\n\nDoctor: Are you making an effort to push away or suppress thoughts or memories of your cousin's death when they come to mind?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm doing that. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
